## PAGE 4
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI/DOJ | **Type:** Explanation of Exemptions Form
**Subject:** Explanation of FOIA and Privacy Act Exemptions

# EXPLANATION OF EXEMPTIONS

### SUBSECTIONS OF TITLE 5, UNITED STATES CODE, SECTION 552

**(b)(1)** (A) specifically authorized under criteria established by an Executive order to be kept secret in the interest of national defense or foreign policy and (B) are in fact properly classified to such Executive order;

**(b)(2)** related solely to the internal personnel rules and practices of an agency;

**(b)(3)** specifically exempted from disclosure by statute (other than section 552b of this title), provided that such statute (A) requires that the matters be withheld from the public in such a manner as to leave no discretion on issue, or (B) establishes particular criteria for withholding or refers to particular types of matters to be withheld;

**(b)(4)** trade secrets and commercial or financial information obtained from a person and privileged or confidential;

**(b)(5)** inter-agency or intra-agency memorandums or letters which would not be available by law to a party other than an agency in litigation with the agency;

**(b)(6)** personnel and medical files and similar files the disclosure of which would constitute a clearly unwarranted invasion of personal privacy;

**(b)(7)** records or information compiled for law enforcement purposes, but only to the extent that the production of such law enforcement records or information ( A ) could reasonably be expected to interfere with enforcement proceedings, ( B ) would deprive a person of a right to a fair trial or an impartial adjudication, ( C ) could reasonably be expected to constitute an unwarranted invasion of personal privacy, ( D ) could reasonably be expected to disclose the identity of confidential source, including a State, local, or foreign agency or authority or any private institution which furnished information on a confidential basis, and, in the case of record or information compiled by a criminal law enforcement authority in the course of a criminal investigation, or by an agency conducting a lawful national security intelligence investigation, information furnished by a confidential source, ( E ) would disclose techniques and procedures for law enforcement investigations or prosecutions, or would disclose guidelines for law enforcement investigations or prosecutions if such disclosure could reasonably be expected to risk circumvention of the law, or ( F ) could reasonably be expected to endanger the life or physical safety of any individual;

**(b)(8)** contained in or related to examination, operating, or condition reports prepared by, on behalf of, or for the use of an agency responsible for the regulation or supervision of financial institutions; or

**(b)(9)** geological and geophysical information and data, including maps, concerning wells.

### SUBSECTIONS OF TITLE 5, UNITED STATES CODE, SECTION 552a

**(d)(5)** information compiled in reasonable anticipation of a civil action proceeding;

**(j)(2)** material reporting investigative efforts pertaining to the enforcement of criminal law including efforts to prevent, control, or reduce crime or apprehend criminals;

**(k)(1)** information which is currently and properly classified pursuant to an Executive order in the interest of the national defense or foreign policy, for example, information involving intelligence sources or methods;

**(k)(2)** investigatory material compiled for law enforcement purposes, other than criminal, which did not result in loss of a right, benefit or privilege under Federal programs, or which would identify a source who furnished information pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence;

**(k)(3)** material maintained in connection with providing protective services to the President of the United States or any other individual pursuant to the authority of Title 18, United States Code, Section 3056;

**(k)(4)** required by statute to be maintained and used solely as statistical records;

**(k)(5)** investigatory material compiled solely for the purpose of determining suitability, eligibility, or qualifications for Federal civilian employment or for access to classified information, the disclosure of which would reveal the identity of the person who furnished information pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence;

**(k)(6)** testing or examination material used to determine individual qualifications for appointment or promotion in Federal Government service the release of which would compromise the testing or examination process;

**(k)(7)** material used to determine potential for promotion in the armed services, the disclosure of which would reveal the identity of the person who furnished the material pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence.

## PAGE 14
**Date:** 1974-12-20 | **Origin:** FBI St. Louis | **Type:** Report
**Subject:** RASHAD ABDEL HALIM KHALIFA

(U)

**CONFIDENTIAL**

[Handwritten bracket around the name]
[Handwritten mark: ~~(S)~~] **RASHAD ABDEL HALIM KHALIFA**

[Margin note: Libya]

On December 3, 1974, the records of the Immigration and Naturalization Service (INS), St. Louis, Missouri, were reviewed by a Special Agent of the Federal Bureau of Investigation and it was noted that a Rashad Abdel Halim Khalifa is assigned INS number A14-177-871. A review of this file revealed that he was admitted to the United States on October 11, 1961 as an exchange student. He attended the University of California at Riverside, California from February of 1962 to June of 1966 and studied horticulture. He then attended the University of Arizona at Tucson, Arizona from June of 1968 to August of 1968 and studied biochemistry. While at Tucson he married a United States citizen, [REDACTED: b6, b7C] [REDACTED: b6, b7C] on January 26, 1963. On [REDACTED: b6, b7C] [REDACTED: b6, b7C] was born at [REDACTED: b6, b7C] ~~(S)~~

On April 15, 1966, he and his family moved from the United States and established residence in the United Arab Republic (UAR) from May 22, 1966 to January 4, 1968. While in the UAR he was employed at the Agrarian Reform Organization, Fruit Division, Cairo, Egypt, as a director from June of 1966 to December of 1967. ~~(S)~~ (U)

In January of 1968 he and his family returned to the United States because they were "politically isolated" and threatened due to the nationality of [REDACTED: b6, b7C]. They settled in St. Louis, Missouri and he was employed with the Monsanto Chemical Corporation, St. Louis, Missouri, as a biochemist from August of 1968 to October of 1970. On [REDACTED: b6, b7C] was born. In October of 1970 he began employment as a chemist with the Anheuser Busch Corporation, St. Louis, Missouri. ~~(S)~~ (U)

According to INS records, Khalifa was naturalized on December 3, 1971. It was also noted in his INS file that he was born on November 18, 1935 at Kafr, Zayat, Egypt. He has Social Security Account Number 546-72-5524 and also spells his last name Khalifah. ~~(S)~~ (U)

On December 20, 1974, a Special Agent of the Federal Bureau of Investigation was advised by the information operator at the American Standards Testing Bureau, St. Louis, Missouri, that Khalifa is currently employed as a chemist at this company. ~~(S)~~ (U)

On September 13, 1985, RASHAD A. KHALIFA, 8825 East 2nd Place, Tucson, Arizona 85710, was interviewed at which time he furnished the following information: ~~(S)~~ (U)

KHALIFA was born on November 18, 1935 at Kafr Zayat, Egypt, and was originally admitted to the United States on October 11, 1961 as an Exchange Student. He became a naturalized citizen of the United States in the United States District Court at St. Louis, Missouri, on December 3, 1971. ~~(S)~~ (U)

KHLAIFA attended the University of California at Riverside, California, from February, 1962 to February, 1966, and studied Horticulture. He then attended the University of Arizona (U of A), Tucson, Arizona, from June, 1968 - August, 1968, and studied Biochemistry. KHALIFA has a Ph.D. degree in chemistry. ~~(S)~~ (U)

KHALIFA advised that since March, 1978, he has resided with [REDACTED: b6, b7C] at 8825 East 2nd Place, Tucson, Arizona 85710. Since April, 1980, KHALIFA has been employed as a Chemist with the ARIZONA COMMISSION OF AGRICULTURE AND HORTICULTURE, Arizona State Chemist's Office. He works at the University of Arizona Experimental Farm located at 1601 West Main, Mesa, Arizona 85201. In addition, KHALIFA is the IMAM (religious leader) at the ISLAMIC MOSQUE of Tucson, 739 East 6th Street, Tucson, Arizona, a place of worship for Muslims. ~~(S)~~ (U)

KHALIFA related that during May, 1985, he learned from public source material that the Iranians were mobilizing along the borders of Iran and Iraq to conduct a major offensive against the Iraqis. This offensive was scheduled to take place during June, 1985, which for Muslims was the holy month known as Ramadan. Ramadan in Islam is the ninth month of the Muhammadan year observed as a sacred month on each day of which strict fasting is practiced from dawn to sunset. Also, Muslims are prohibited from engaging in fighting or acts of violence during Ramadan and fighting is only allowed during this holy month solely for purposes of self-defense. ~~(S)~~ (U)

In this regard KHALIFA traveled to the IRANIAN MISSION to the United Nations (IMUN) in late May, 1985, and met with the Iranian Ambassador to the United Nations, SAID RAJAIE KHORASSANI. KHALIFA discussed with KHORASSANI the fact that the Iranians were mobilizing against the Iraqis on the borders of Iran and Iraq. ~~(S)~~ (U)

~~SECRET~~

4

---

(U)

$$\text{SECRET}$$

PX
APM:mdb [REDACTED]

b3
b7E

KHALIFA beseeched KHORASSANI to intercede and arrange for a dicontinuance of these plans for a major military offensive. KHALIFA informed KHORASSANI that KHALIFA'S intentions were to save the lives of 10,000 young men, who would be killed during such a military operation. KHALIFA also reminded KHORASSANI that in addition it was completely against the religious faith of Muslims, as set forth in the KORAN, to engage in any fighting during the holy month of Ramadan. KHALIFA noted that the specific dates for Ramadan vary from year to year and in 1985 Ramadan ran from June 12, 1985 - July 12, 1985. KHALIFA said that much to his shock and dismay KHORASSANI said he would not only not stop the mobilization efforts but that rather than 10,000 young men, 100,000 young men would have to die to bring about the coming of MAHDI. MAHDI is believed by Muslims to be literally the 12th grandson of the prophet MUHAMMAD. MAHDI is the expected spiritual and temporal ruler destined to establish a reign of righteousness throughout the world.
(U)

Disappointed and frustrated over this meeting with KHORASSANI, KHALIFA returned to Tucson, Arizona. He shortly thereafter traveled to Vancouver, Canada, to visit with [REDACTED] [REDACTED] is a professional baseball player with [REDACTED] who were playing the VANCOUVER CANADIANS BASEBALL TEAM at the time. While KHALIFA was in Vancouver, he received a telephone call from [REDACTED] at the ISLAMIC MOSQUE of Tucson. [REDACTED] advised KHALIFA that KHALIFA had received a telephone call from [REDACTED] of the IMUN. [REDACTED] had stated to [REDACTED] that there was a very important message for KHALIFA at the IMUN and that KHALIFA would have to pick up this message at the IMUN in person.
(U)

[REDACTED: b6, b7C]

KHALIFA thereupon telephoned [REDACTED] at the IMUN from Vancouver Airport and was advised by [REDACTED] that there was an important message from the Iranian Government at the IMUN. [REDACTED] further advised KHALIFA that [REDACTED] could not give KHALIFA the message over the telephone, but that KHALIFA would have to travel to the IMUN in New York City to receive the message in person. KHALIFA requested the IMUN pay for the airplane ticket to New York City and [REDACTED] refused, saying that KHALIFA would have to pay his own way if he wanted the message.
(U)

[REDACTED: b6, b7C]

KHALIFA said he did not therefore go to the IMUN and never received the message from the Iranian Government. KHALIFA has no idea of the substance of the message. KHALIFA never heard from [REDACTED] KHORASSANI, or the IMUN since that time and has no plans for any further contact with the IMUN.
(U)

[REDACTED: b6, b7C]

KHALIFA advised that he knew of no Middle East violence prone individuals or organizations in the Tucson, Arizona area. KHALIFA further advised he knew of no individuals in the Tucson area, who were conducting activities on behalf of Iran.
(U)

Dissemination Section
Information Management Division

Enclosure(s)

In response to your negotiated FOIA request, enclosed are the processed documents. This is the **first**
**interim release** of information responsive to your FOIA request. Duplicate copies of the same document were not
processed. This material is being provided to you at no charge.

As previously indicated, documents were located which originated with, or contained information concerning,
other agencies. We are consulting with the other agencies and are awaiting their response. Our office has
processed all other information currently in our possession. The FBI will correspond with you regarding those
documents when the consultation is completed.

## PAGE 3
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI | **Type:** Addendum
**Subject:** FBI FOIA Addendum

**FBI FOIA Addendum**

**As referenced in our letter responding to your Freedom of Information/Privacy Acts (FOIPA) request, the FBI FOIPA Addendum provides information applicable to your request. Part 1 of the Addendum includes standard responses that apply to all requests. Part 2 includes standard responses that apply to requests for records about individuals to the extent your request seeks the listed information. Part 3 includes general information about FBI records, searches, and programs.**

**Part 1: The standard responses below apply to all requests:**

**(i)** 5 U.S.C. § 552(c). Congress excluded three categories of law enforcement and national security records from the requirements of the FOIPA [5 U.S.C. § 552(c)]. FBI responses are limited to those records subject to the requirements of the FOIPA. Additional information about the FBI and the FOIPA can be found on the www.fbi.gov/foia website.

**(ii)** Intelligence Records. To the extent your request seeks records of intelligence sources, methods, or activities, the FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of records pursuant to FOIA exemptions (b)(1), (b)(3), and as applicable to requests for records about individuals, PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(1), (b)(3), and (j)(2)]. The mere acknowledgment of the existence or nonexistence of such records itself is a classified fact protected by FOIA exemption (b)(1) and/or would reveal intelligence sources, methods, or activities protected by exemption (b)(3) [50 USC § 3024(i)(1)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that any such records do or do not exist.

**Part 2: The standard responses below apply to all requests for records on individuals:**

**(i)** Requests for Records about any Individual—Watch Lists. The FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of any individual's name on a watch list pursuant to FOIA exemption (b)(7)(E) and PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(7)(E), (j)(2)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that watch list records do or do not exist.

**(ii)** Requests for Records about any Individual—Witness Security Program Records. The FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of records which could identify any participant in the Witness Security Program pursuant to FOIA exemption (b)(3) and PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(3), 18 U.S.C. 3521, and (j)(2)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that such records do or do not exist.

**(iii)** Requests for Records for Incarcerated Individuals. The FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of records which could reasonably be expected to endanger the life or physical safety of any incarcerated individual pursuant to FOIA exemptions (b)(7)(E), (b)(7)(F), and PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(7)(E), (b)(7)(F), and (j)(2)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that such records do or do not exist.

**Part 3: General Information:**

**(i)** Record Searches. The Record/Information Dissemination Section (RIDS) searches for reasonably described records by searching systems or locations where responsive records would reasonably be found. A standard search normally consists of a search for main files in the Central Records System (CRS), an extensive system of records consisting of applicant, investigative, intelligence, personnel, administrative, and general files compiled by the FBI per its law enforcement, intelligence, and administrative functions. The CRS spans the entire FBI organization, comprising records of FBI Headquarters, FBI Field Offices, and FBI Legal Attaché Offices (Legats) worldwide; Electronic Surveillance (ELSUR) records are included in the CRS. Unless specifically requested, a standard search does not include references, administrative records of previous FOIPA requests, or civil litigation files. For additional information about our record searches, visit www.fbi.gov/services/information-management/foipa/requesting-fbi-records.

**(ii)** FBI Records. Founded in 1908, the FBI carries out a dual law enforcement and national security mission. As part of this dual mission, the FBI creates and maintains records on various subjects; however, the FBI does not maintain records on every person, subject, or entity.

**(iii)** Requests for Criminal History Records or Rap Sheets. The Criminal Justice Information Services (CJIS) Division provides Identity History Summary Checks – often referred to as a criminal history record or rap sheet. These criminal history records are not the same as material in an investigative “FBI file.” An Identity History Summary Check is a listing of information taken from fingerprint cards and documents submitted to the FBI in connection with arrests, federal employment, naturalization, or military service. For a fee, individuals can request a copy of their Identity History Summary Check. Forms and directions can be accessed at www.fbi.gov/about-us/cjis/identity-history-summary-checks. Additionally, requests can be submitted electronically at www.edo.cjis.gov. For additional information, please contact CJIS directly at (304) 625-5500.

**(iv)** National Name Check Program (NNCP). The mission of NNCP is to analyze and report information in response to name check requests received from federal agencies, for the purpose of protecting the United States from foreign and domestic threats to national security. Please be advised that this is a service provided to other federal agencies. Private Citizens cannot request a name check.

## PAGE 4
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI | **Type:** Explanation of Exemptions
**Subject:** Explanations of FOIA Exemptions under Title 5, United States Code, Section 552 and Section 552a

**EXPLANATION OF EXEMPTIONS**

**SUBSECTIONS OF TITLE 5, UNITED STATES CODE, SECTION 552**

(b)(1) (A) specifically authorized under criteria established by an Executive order to be kept secret in the interest of national defense or foreign policy and (B) are in fact properly classified to such Executive order;

(b)(2) related solely to the internal personnel rules and practices of an agency;

(b)(3) specifically exempted from disclosure by statute (other than section 552b of this title), provided that such statute (A) requires that the matters be withheld from the public in such a manner as to leave no discretion on issue, or (B) establishes particular criteria for withholding or refers to particular types of matters to be withheld;

(b)(4) trade secrets and commercial or financial information obtained from a person and privileged or confidential;

(b)(5) inter-agency or intra-agency memorandums or letters which would not be available by law to a party other than an agency in litigation with the agency;

(b)(6) personnel and medical files and similar files the disclosure of which would constitute a clearly unwarranted invasion of personal privacy;

(b)(7) records or information compiled for law enforcement purposes, but only to the extent that the production of such law enforcement records or information ( A ) could reasonably be expected to interfere with enforcement proceedings, ( B ) would deprive a person of a right to a fair trial or an impartial adjudication, ( C ) could reasonably be expected to constitute an unwarranted invasion of personal privacy, ( D ) could reasonably be expected to disclose the identity of confidential source, including a State, local, or foreign agency or authority or any private institution which furnished information on a confidential basis, and, in the case of record or information compiled by a criminal law enforcement authority in the course of a criminal investigation, or by an agency conducting a lawful national security intelligence investigation, information furnished by a confidential source, ( E ) would disclose techniques and procedures for law enforcement investigations or prosecutions, or would disclose guidelines for law enforcement investigations or prosecutions if such disclosure could reasonably be expected to risk circumvention of the law, or ( F ) could reasonably be expected to endanger the life or physical safety of any individual;

(b)(8) contained in or related to examination, operating, or condition reports prepared by, on behalf of, or for the use of an agency responsible for the regulation or supervision of financial institutions; or

(b)(9) geological and geophysical information and data, including maps, concerning wells.

**SUBSECTIONS OF TITLE 5, UNITED STATES CODE, SECTION 552a**

(d)(5) information compiled in reasonable anticipation of a civil action proceeding;

(j)(2) material reporting investigative efforts pertaining to the enforcement of criminal law including efforts to prevent, control, or reduce crime or apprehend criminals;

(k)(1) information which is currently and properly classified pursuant to an Executive order in the interest of the national defense or foreign policy, for example, information involving intelligence sources or methods;

(k)(2) investigatory material compiled for law enforcement purposes, other than criminal, which did not result in loss of a right, benefit or privilege under Federal programs, or which would identify a source who furnished information pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence;

(k)(3) material maintained in connection with providing protective services to the President of the United States or any other individual pursuant to the authority of Title 18, United States Code, Section 3056;

(k)(4) required by statute to be maintained and used solely as statistical records;

(k)(5) investigatory material compiled solely for the purpose of determining suitability, eligibility, or qualifications for Federal civilian employment or for access to classified information, the disclosure of which would reveal the identity of the person who furnished information pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence;

(k)(6) testing or examination material used to determine individual qualifications for appointment or promotion in Federal Government service the release of which would compromise the testing or examination process;

(k)(7) material used to determine potential for promotion in the armed services, the disclosure of which would reveal the identity of the person who furnished the material pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence.

FBI/DOJ

JANUARY 31, 1990; POSSIBLE TERRORIST ACT; AOT/DT; OO: PHOENIX.

RE DENVER TELETYPE TO DIRECTOR DATED OCTOBER 30, 1989 AND CAPTIONED "FUQRA; DOMESTIC SECURITY - TERRORISM DES/T (OO: DENVER)."

THE FOLLOWING INFORMATION IS BEING PROVIDED TO RECEIVING OFFICES DUE TO THE POSSIBILITY THAT THE ORGANIZATION FUQRA MIGHT HAVE BEEN INVOLVED IN THE MURDER OF KHALIFA.

FOR BACKGROUND INFORMATION, KHALIFA WAS MURDERED ON JANUARY 31, 1990, SOMEWHERE BETWEEN THE HOURS OF 2:00 A.M. AND 5:00 A.M. BY UNKNOWN ASSAILANT(S) AT THE MASJID OR MOSQUE OF TUCSON, 729 EAST 6TH STREET, TUCSON, ARIZONA. KHALIFA WAS THE IMAN AND FOUNDER OF THE MOSQUE OF TUCSON AND A MODERATE MOSLEM. HE WAS ALSO WELL KNOWN AMONG MOSLEMS BECAUSE OF COMPUTER ANALYSIS HE DID ON THE KORAN THAT SHOWED A MATHEMATICAL FORMULA THAT HE BELIEVED PROVED THE KORAN IS A DIVINE SCRIPTURE. KHALIFA ALSO PUBLISHED A TRANSLATION OF THE KORAN IN WHICH HE OMITTED TWO VERSES THAT HE BELIEVED WERE SATANIC. FURTHERMORE, KHALIFA ANGERED OTHER MOSLEMS BY ACCUSING THEM OF FOLLOWING THE SATANIC KORAN. KHALIFA WAS ALSO HATED BY MANY MAINSTREAM MOSLEMS FOR CLAIMING THAT HE WAS THE MESSENGER OF GOD.

PAGE TWO DE FBIPX 0007 UNCLAS

SUBJECT:  MURDER OF RASHAD ABDEL KHALIFA AT TUCSON, ARIZONA ON

JANUARY 31, 1990; POSSIBLE TERRORIST ACT; AOT/DT; OO:  PHOENIX.

    RE DENVER TELETYPE TO DIRECTOR DATED OCTOBER 30, 1989 AND

CAPTIONED "FUQRA; DOMESTIC SECURITY - TERRORISM DES/T (OO:

DENVER)."

    THE FOLLOWING INFORMATION IS BEING PROVIDED TO RECEIVING

OFFICES DUE TO THE POSSIBILITY THAT THE ORGANIZATION FUQRA MIGHT

HAVE BEEN INVOLVED IN THE MURDER OF KHALIFA.

    FOR BACKGROUND INFORMATION, KHALIFA WAS MURDERED ON JANUARY

31, 1990, SOMEWHERE BETWEEN THE HOURS OF 2:00 A.M. AND 5:00 A.M.

BY UNKNOWN ASSAILANT(S) AT THE MASJID OR MOSQUE OF TUCSON, 729

EAST 6TH STREET, TUCSON, ARIZONA.  KHALIFA WAS THE IMAN AND

FOUNDER OF THE MOSQUE OF TUCSON AND A MODERATE MOSLEM.  HE WAS

ALSO WELL KNOWN AMONG MOSLEMS BECAUSE OF COMPUTER ANALYSIS HE DID

ON THE KORAN THAT SHOWED A MATHEMATICAL FORMULA THAT HE BELIEVED

PROVED THE KORAN IS A DIVINE SCRIPTURE.  KHALIFA ALSO PUBLISHED A

TRANSLATION OF THE KORAN IN WHICH HE OMITTED TWO VERSES THAT HE

BELIEVED WERE SATANIC.  FURTHERMORE, KHALIFA ANGERED OTHER

MOSLEMS BY ACCUSING THEM OF FOLLOWING THE SATANIC KORAN.  KHALIFA

WAS ALSO HATED BY MANY MAINSTREAM MOSLEMS FOR CLAIMING THAT HE

WAS THE MESSENGER OF GOD.

---


## PAGE 18
**Date:** 1990-01-31 | **Origin:** FBI Phoenix | **Type:** Teletype
**Subject:** Investigation into the homicide of Khalifa and possible involvement of Fuqra

PAGE THREE DE FBIPX 0007 UNCLAS

ON JANUARY 31, 1990, TUCSON POLICE DEPARTMENT CONTACTED TUCSON RA AND REQUESTED THE FBI'S ASSISTANCE IN THE HOMICIDE DUE TO THE POSSIBILITY THAT KHALIFA WAS SLAIN FOR POLITICAL OR RELIGIOUS REASONS. ON THIS SAME DATE, TUCSON POLICE DEPARTMENT PROVIDED PHOENIX WITH A PACKET OF INFORMATION DEALING WITH A MILITANT BLACK MUSLIM SECT KNOWN AS FUQRA THAT MIGHT HAVE BEEN INVOLVED WITH THE MURDER. MEMBERS OF FUQRA PROFESS THAT THEY ARE THE FRONT RUNNERS FOR THE ENTIRE ISLAMIC WORLD AND THAT SUCH GROUPS AS THE AHMADIYYA MOVEMENT IN ISLAM (AMI) AND OTHER MOSLEM GROUPS WHO DIFFER FROM THEIR BELIEFS AND PHILOSOPHIES ARE THEIR ENEMIES. THESE OTHER GROUPS ALSO INCLUDE ISRAEL, THE NATION OF ISLAM, ANSAR MUSLEMS, JEWISH DEFENSE LEAGUE, HARE KRISHNAS, AND THE UNITED STATES GOVERNMENT.

THE TUCSON POLICE DEPARTMENT RECEIVED THE INFORMATION REGARDING FUQRA FROM THE COLORADO SPRINGS POLICE DEPARTMENT (CSPD) IN COLORADO SPRINGS, COLORADO. ACCORDING TO TUCSON POLICE DEPARTMENT, ON SEPTEMBER 14, 1989, CSPD SEARCHED A STORAGE LOCKER BELONGING TO FUQRA. INSIDE THE STORAGE LOCKER, CSPD DISCOVERED SEVERAL EXPLOSIVE COMPONENTS, 30 POUNDS OF EXPLOSIVES, THREE LARGE PIPE BOMBS, A NUMBER OF SMALLER IMPROVISED EXPLOSIVE

---


## PAGE 19
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI Phoenix (FBIPX) | **Type:** Teletype
**Subject:** Fuqra group search results, surveillance and terrorist plotting materials recovered in storage locker

PAGE FOUR DE FBIPX 0007 UNCLAS

DEVICES, SHAPE CHARGES, 10 HANDGUNS, SEVERAL WITH SERIAL NUMBERS
OBLITERATED, MILITARY TRAINING MANUALS, HANDWRITTEN NOTES ON BOMB
CONSTRUCTION, AND RELATED MATTERS, INCLUDING A SOMEWHAT DETAILED
DESCRIPTION OF A BOMB ATTACK AGAINST THE FACILITY IN DENVER.
THERE WERE ALSO EXTENSIVE WRITINGS LINKING THE GROUP TO A "HOLY
WAR IN THE UNITED STATES AGAINST PERCEIVED RELIGIOUS AND ETHNIC
ENEMIES." INSIDE THE STORAGE LOCKER WERE ALSO HANDWRITTEN
INSTRUCTIONAL MATERIAL DEALING WITH VARIOUS ASPECTS OF GUERRILLA
WARFARE, TERRORIST BOMBINGS, SNIPING, TARGET SELECTION AND
SURVEILLANCE. EXTENSIVE PACKETS OF APPARENT TARGETING
INFORMATION WERE ALSO FOUND, WHICH DEALT WITH SURVEILLANCE OF
SEVERAL APPARENT POTENTIAL TARGETS IN DENVER, TUCSON, ARIZONA,
AND THE LOS ANGELES AREA. EACH OF THESE PACKETS CONTAINED
DETAILED SURVEILLANCE REPORTS, DIAGRAMS, PHOTOGRAPHS, AND
IDENTIFYING DATA ON EACH TARGET, AS IF IT WERE BEING SCOUTED FOR
A TERRORIST ATTACK. THE LANGUAGE USED IN THE REPORTS MADE IT
CLEAR THAT SOME TYPE OF VIOLENT ACTION WAS ANTICIPATED. ALL OF
THE REPORTS SEEM TO DATE FROM AROUND MID-1984, AND THERE WERE NO
PACKETS WHICH SEEMED TO BE MORE RECENT.

A REVIEW OF THE FUQRA PACKET FOR TUCSON REVEALED THAT THERE
WAS A MOSQUE RAID PLANNED FOR THE TUCSON MOSQUE, SUPRA. FROM A

---


## PAGE 20
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI Phoenix (FBIPX) | **Type:** Teletype
**Subject:** Target information packet regarding Khalifa and Tucson incident

PAGE FIVE DE FBIPX 0007 UNCLAS

FURTHER REVIEW OF THIS PACKET, IT APPEARS THAT KHALIFA WAS THE PROBABLE TARGET FOR THIS ATTACK. THIS PACKET FOR TUCSON WAS VERY DETAILED AND INCLUDED DESCRIPTIONS OF THE MOSQUE AS WELL AS A STACK OF 35 MILLIMETER COLOR PHOTOGRAPHS OF THE MOSQUE FROM ALL ANGLES, AS WELL AS INTERIOR SHOTS. THERE WERE ALSO DETAILS ABOUT THE NEIGHBORHOOD, BEST METHODS TO BE USED TO KILL THE INDIVIDUAL, AND THE BEST TIME TO DO IT. ONE SECTION OF THE PACKAGE WAS ALSO ENTITLED "FURTHER INFO FOR FINAL SETUP." UNDER THIS INFORMATION, IT SAYS THAT A PARTICIPANT WOULD GO THE MOSQUE ONE WEEK BEFORE THE EVENT, ATTEND CLASSES THERE, AND DETERMINE THE AVERAGE CROWD SIZE AS WELL AS MOVEMENTS OF THE SUBJECT. NEAR THE END OF THE PLAN IS A SECTION ON "PROBLEMS" AND IT STATES "HE MAY NOT BE THERE. PROPOSED SOLUTION IS AS WE WAIT, EVERYONE WHO COMES IN MUST BE ELIMINATED UNTIL HE SHOWS UP. IF FOR SOME REASON HE DOESN'T, THEN THAT'S ALL WE CAN DO."

IT SHOULD BE NOTED THAT CIRCUMSTANCES SURROUNDING THE MURDER OF KHALIFA ARE VERY SIMILAR TO THE FUQRA TARGET INFORMATION PACKET FOUND FOR TUCSON. IT IS ALSO OF INTEREST TO NOTE THAT THE GROUP'S [REDACTED: b6, b7C] DATE OF BIRTH [REDACTED: b6, b7C] RELEASED FROM PRISON ON [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

**Date:** 1990-02-01 | **Origin:** FBI | **Type:** Teletype
**Subject:** Fuqra, Murder of Khalifa, Phoenix Investigation

PAGE SIX DE FBIPX 0007 UNCLAS

[REDACTED: b6, b7C] IS BELIEVED TO BE [REDACTED: b6, b7C] FOR THIS GROUP AND WAS CONVICTED FOR HIS PARTICIPATION IN [REDACTED: b6, b7C] [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

ADDITIONAL INFORMATION ON FUQRA CAN BE FOUND IN DENVER TELETYPE TO DIRECTOR AND ALL OFFICES DATED OCTOBER 30, 1989.

ON FEBRUARY 1, 1990, TUCSON POLICE DEPARTMENT ADVISED THE FBI THAT INTERVIEWS WITH MEMBERS OF THE MOSQUE HAVE REVEALED THAT A NEW INDIVIDUAL WAS SEEN HANGING AROUND THE MOSQUE A COUPLE OF WEEKS AGO. THIS INDIVIDUAL WAS ATTENDING PRAYER SESSIONS, AND CLAIMED HE WAS FROM [REDACTED: b6, b7C] MEMBERS DESCRIBE THE INDIVIDUAL AS A [REDACTED: b6, b7C] MALE, APPROXIMATELY [REDACTED: b6, b7C] MEMBERS ALSO INDICATED THAT THE MAN WAS SEEN DRIVING A VEHICLE, NOT FURTHER IDENTIFIED, WITH AN OREGON PLATE. AROUND THE PLATE WERE THE WORDS [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

SINCE THERE ARE SIMILARITIES IN THE FUQRA PLAN AND THE CIRCUMSTANCES SURROUNDING THE MURDER OF KHALIFA, PHOENIX IS OPENING A NEW INVESTIGATION TO DETERMINE IF A POSSIBLE TERRORIST ACT HAS OCCURRED. THEREFORE, RECEIVING OFFICES ARE REQUESTED TO TASK LOGICAL ASSETS ON ANY INFORMATION ON FUQRA AND THE MURDER OF BT

### Muslim leader found slain in Tucson mosque

**By Sam Negri**
*The Arizona Republic*

**TUCSON** — The leader of a Muslim splinter group was found slain Wednesday in a mosque near the University of Arizona, and police said he may have been killed because of his vehement criticism of traditional Muslims.

Rashad Khalifa, 54, was found by his secretary at 5:45 a.m. on the kitchen floor of the United Submitters International Mosque, according to Tucson police Detective Charles Armijo.

"He had several injuries," Armijo said, "and until an autopsy is performed, we aren't saying what the cause was. But we are considering this a homicide."

Armijo said police have not ruled out the possibility that Khalifa was the victim of warring factions within the Muslim community.

*— See MUSLIM, page A16*

---

### Muslim leader found killed in Tucson

*— **MUSLIM**, from page A1*

nity.

An assistant of Khalifa, Edip Yuksel, reportedly said he was "90 percent sure" that Khalifa had been killed by assassins with religious ties.

Yuksel said Khalifa was prominent in Muslim circles and was not hard to find, coming to the mosque at 2 a.m. daily to study the Koran and to write, the *Tucson Citizen* reported Wednesday.

Yuksel told the newspaper, "He knew he would be killed" because of his research.

Police at first reported Khalifa had been shot.

A police spokesman later said, "The type of trauma found on the body made it unclear what the cause of death was."

Investigators said there were no signs of forced entry and that the main door of the building was open when officers arrived.

Members of the mosque, who followed Khalifa's interpretation of the Koran, described themselves as "Submitters."

The extent of his following could not be determined immediately. Members of the congregation said they planned to issue a statement about their beliefs and the slaying.

The group's brochure states: "A Submitter is one who devotes his or her worship absolutely to God alone — no idols whatever. A Submitter... may be Jewish, Christian, Muslim, Hindu, Buddhist, etc."

The brochure states most religions have strayed from their original teachings and that "today's Muslims have corrupted such teachings beyond recognition."

In an interview with the *Tucson Citizen* last year, Khalifa said he had proved through a 19-year computer study that all major components of the Koran, from verses to chapters, were composed in accordance with a mathematical code that had the common denominator of 19. He contended that the code proved that God, and not man wrote, the Koran, Islam's holy writings.

"The code proved that many Muslims were following satanic guidance. They were distorting the religion by following prophets that were men instead of God," Khalifa told the *Citizen*.

The newspaper reported that Khalifa, known as an imam, or prayer leader, was intent upon widely disseminating his discovery. He did so, by transmitting his writings by facsimile machine throughout the Middle East.

Contributing to this report was *The Associated Press*.

# Mosque leader is found slain

## Tucsonan was focus of religious uproar

### By Carmen Duarte
The Arizona Daily Star

An internationally renowned and controversial Islamic leader was killed early yesterday morning in a Tucson mosque.

Imam Rashad Khalifa, 54, the founder and spiritual leader of the Masjid, or Mosque of, Tucson, 739 E. Sixth St., was found "in a kitchen area" by the mosque's secretary, authorities said.

Officials said Khalifa had received death threats because of his interpretation of the Koran, the sacred book of Moslems.

Khalifa, according to friends and associates, caused anger and hatred in the Islamic community because of his interpretation of the scriptures.

And a police homicide sergeant said there is a possibility his death may be connected to political or religious problems.

### FBI involvement

The FBI was called in to help the Tucson Police Department because of the possibility Khalifa was slain because of political or religious beliefs.

Police responded to the mosque at 5:45 a.m. after a report of a shooting, authorities said.

Police would not confirm if Khalifa, who once was a science adviser to Libyan leader Col. Moammar Gadhafi, was shot or say what type of wounds Khalifa suffered.

Authorities said Khalifa "died a violent death."

The body was taken to the Pima County Medical Examiner's Office, where an autopsy was scheduled for today.

### "Number of avenues"

"There is a possibility that motives (for the slaying) may include political and religious problems," said homicide Sgt. Charles Armijo of the Tucson Police Department.

"There are a number of avenues we're pursuing," Armijo said. "We are not limiting ourselves to any one specific lead or area at this point."

FBI Special Agent Larry Bagley, supervisor of the Tucson office, said agents will coordinate out-of-state leads and that the agency has offered police its laboratory services.

"Khalifa is a moderate Moslem, who's made interpretations of the Koran that has angered many Mid-eastern Moslems," said a law enforcement official who asked not to be identified.

"He's been a high-profile person who has received death threats in the past," the official said.

Khalifa, an imam, or spiritual leader of the mosque, made a computer analysis of the Koran and wrote books about his findings.

Edip Yuksel, who was an assistant to Khalifa, said Khalifa arrived at the mosque at about 2 or 3 a.m. every day to work on his religious writings and translations of the Koran.

He said Khalifa, who founded the mosque, was well-known among Moslems because of a computer analysis of the Koran that showed a mathematical formula that Khalifa said proved that the Koran is divine scripture.

Khalifa wrote books about his finding, and also published a translation of the Koran in which he omitted two satanic verses, said a friend of Khalifa's who asked not to be identified.

Khalifa accused other Moslems of following a satanic Koran, an accusation that caused anger toward Khalifa and hatred of him, said the friend.

Khalifa also claimed to be a messenger of God, and mainstream Moslems believe that Mohammed was the last messenger, the friend said.

Anne Betteridge, a University of Arizona Middle Eastern studies professor, felt that all Moslems are Moslems,” said Betteridge, executive secretary of the Middle East Association of North America.

Yuksel said there were about 300 owers who worshiped at the sque.

“We’re all sorry about his death,” Yuksel said. “We believe in the resurrection. We believe he went to heaven.”

A few followers gathered yesterday afternoon and mourned at the mosque, which had a message on one of its walls that read: “Happiness is submission to God.”

Khalifa had lived in Tucson more than 20 years. He moved to Tucson from Tanta, Egypt, to attend the University of Arizona. He received a doctorate in biochemistry from the University of California, where he taught for two years before spending six years in industrial research.

In 1976, he was a science adviser to Gadhafi, and later was imprisoned by the Libyan leader, said Yuksel.

Yuksel said some of Khalifa’s friends were executed and that Khalifa was released from prison.

Yuksel also said Khalifa worked as a technical expert for the United Nations Industrial Development Organization, which promotes industrialization of developing countries.

Khalifa was married. His survivors include a son and daughter.

His son, Sammy, was an infielder in the Pittsburgh Pirates organization from 1982 until November, when the Pirates dropped him from their 40-man roster.

NBC sports commentator Ahmad Rashad was a pupil of Khalifa’s.

Rashad, who formerly was known as Bobby Moore, played 10 years in the National Football League with the St. Louis Cardinals, the Buffalo Bills and the Minnesota Vikings. He ended his career as a wide receiver in 1982.

Rashad has a weekly, nationally syndicated 30-minute television program called “In Sport,” described by an NBC spokesman yesterday as a show with a magazine format that focuses on sports entertainment.

Attempts to reach Rashad for comment yesterday were unsuccessful.

Stephanie Robinson, who identified herself as Rashad’s assistant, said from her Washington, D.C., office yesterday that Rashad was “on location, filming” somewhere in California.

He used a computer to find a numerical code in the Koran based on the number 19 to show that the Koran is a divine scripture and God's message to the world, said Edit Yuksel, an associate of Khalifa's.

Khalifa's research began more than 20 years ago.

Khalifa's message will continue to be spread around the world according to God's will, read a prepared statement from worshipers at the mosque.

Khalifa, who moved to Tucson from Tanta, Egypt, more than 20 years ago, was a science adviser to Libyan leader Col. Moammar Gadhafi, according to a friend.

Arik said the FBI contacted the Tucson Police Department about three days before the killing about a murder plot.

Arik said the plot to kill Khalifa originated in Salt Lake City and that he believed someone was arrested. He said police warned Khalifa about the plot.

Armijo denied that police told Khalifa about the plot.

Yesterday, FBI Special Agent James Screen, of Salt Lake City, and officers of other law enforcement agencies there denied knowing about a plot to kill Khalifa.

FBI Special Agent Larry Bagley, supervisor of the Tucson office, also denied knowing about the plot.

"We have not contacted the Police Department or Khalifa about any recent threats," Bagley said.

A law enforcement official, who did not want to be identified, said that several years ago, a plot to kill Khalifa was discovered. But the plot did not originate in Salt Lake City, the official added.

The official said that "some other agencies" knew about the plot, and that the Tucson Police Department was given the information.

"It may or may not be related to what happened now," the official said. "We will be looking into it."

Bagley said FBI agents will conduct out-of-state interviews on the case for the Tucson Police Department. He would not comment further.

Tucson police Lt. Anthony Day-kin, community services division commander, said police are working with the FBI in gathering "intelligence on reports of (death) threats."

He said because of the "possible international implications the investigation will take some time."

# Khalifa warned before killing

**By CHARLOTTE LOWE and DAVID L. TEIBEL**
*Citizen Staff Writers*

Police uncovered an apparent murder conspiracy three days before the Tucson stabbing death of internationally known Moslem leader Rashad Khalifa, his sucessor [sic] said yesterday.

Police said Khalifa, 54, was stabbed numerous times before dawn yesterday. "The weapon used was not found, but police said they did find a handgun under Khalifa's body.

The case may have international implications. The FBI and a police special investigations unit that monitors some Mideast affairs here have been called into the investigation.

Abdullah Arik was Khalifa's assistant and now replaces him as leader of a controversial moderate Moslem sect based at the Masjid (Mosque) of Tucson, 739 E. Sixth St., near the University of Arizona.

Arik said the most recent of many death threats against Khalifa was uncovered three days ago in Salt Lake City.

### Warned to be careful

"They found someone with a picture of the mosque and evidence of some intention to do harm, to blow the mosque up. I believe they arrested the person and they warned Dr. Khalifa to be careful," Arik said.

The Islamic leader's controversial religious beliefs and "the way he rewrote the Koran" made him "a Salman Rushdie" to many other more conservative sects, Detective Sgt. Charles Armijo said yesterday.

Rushdie wrote the controversial book "The Satanic Verses" and a year ago, Iranian leader Ayatollah Ruhollah Khomeini urged his followers to hunt down Rushdie and kill him., Rushdie has remained in hiding since then.

### Not afraid of being killed

Last year, Khalifa said he was not afraid of being killed. "God promises to guide and protect his messengers. No one kills. The time comes and God takes."

Arik said that as new leader of the group, his own safety does not concern him. "I'm more worried about pulling these people out of this situation and delivering the message he (Khalifa) intended to deliver to the world," Arik said.

Khalifa's followers believed he was a messenger of God, Arik said. Yesterday, they felt as Christians would if Jesus would come to Earth and be killed, Arik said.

The congregation will be allowed to reenter the mosque for daily prayers today, said Armijo.

"We're concerned about (the mosque). It's also a target, being that it represents his teachings," he said.

### Followers mourn together

* Last night, Khalifa's followers prayed in an apartment next to the mosque where he died.
* A refrigerator and floor remained smeared with his blood in the room beside the sanctuary where he taught his faithful with the words of the Koran, the holy book of the Islamic people.
* Next door, in a small apartment, mourners gathered to comfort each other. They sat crosslegged in a circle and ate takeout fried chicken. Outside, their shoes, removed according to Moslem custom, filled with rain.
* The mourners were among about 55 Tucsonans to whom Khalifa taught the beliefs that caused his death, said one of his followers, who wished not to be named. Throughout the world, there were thousands who called him their leader, Khalifa said in an interview last March.

Khalifa believed in the worship of one God, the equality of all people and religions and literal translation of the Koran rather than interpretation based on cultural tradition.

## PAGE 49
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** Unknown | **Type:** Newspaper Clipping
**Subject:** Murder investigation of Rashad Khalifa in Tucson, Arizona

Police Chief Peter Ronstadt late yesterday said of the killing, "We have more questions than we have answers."

**No definite leads on motive**
"To the best of my knowledge, there's no definite information on motive," Ronstadt said.
He would not comment on any evidence that may have been found inside the mosque.
Armijo said the FBI has been called into the case.
"We have an FBI agent working with us because of the possibility of political, religious motives," Armijo said. "They are doing a number of things for us, including tracking down leads out of state."
Asked if anyone had called police or the media to claim responsibility for the killing, Armijo said, "not to my knowledge."

**FBI to cooperate with TPD**
Larry Bagley, in charge of the FBI's Tucson office, would say only that the FBI yesterday conducted an out-of-state interview on behalf of Tucson Police. He said the FBI will make available to Tucson police any intelligence, laboratory or investigative resources needed in the investigation.
Bagley did not elaborate on the interview conducted by the FBI and he would not comment on possible motives in the Khalifa murder.
Khalifa came to the mosque every day about 2 a.m. to do religious research and to write, said his assistant, Edip Yuksel.
Police said Khalifa's secretary came to work about 5:30 a.m. yesterday to pray with Khalifa before beginning work.

**Secretary discoverd body**
She discovered the body and called police, reporting that there had been a shooting.
Armijo said Khalifa was stabbed numerous times. He would not comment on whether any evidence was found at the scene that would lead police to a suspect or a motive.
Armijo also said a .22-caliber gun was found under Khalifa's body. "We think it was his," Armijo said. "He must have had it with him."
Khalifa, through his research, claimed to have discovered two satanic verses in the Koran, said his assistant, Yuksel.
Yuksel said Khalifa published a version of the Koran that omitted those verses and then went on to claim that all other Moslems were following a satanic version of the Koran.
That, Yuksel said, was followed by death threats from around the Moslem world.

**'Knew he would be killed'**
Yuksel said he was "90 percent sure" Khalifa was killed for his religious beliefs.
"He knew he would be killed," Yuksel said.
Yuksel, who is translating Khalifa's version of the Koran into his native Turkish language, said he also has received death threats.
A friend of Khalifa's, who had asked not to be identified, said Khalifa, feeling he had to spread word of his beliefs, used a facsimile machine to send his writings throughout the Middle East, thus generating further controversy.
The friend said Khalifa further angered other Moslems by referring to himself as the Messenger of God, a title other Moslems accord only to Muhammed.
But Armijo said that a religiously motivated assassination is only one of a number of possibilities being investigated in the slaying.
A friend of Khalifa's said he had "trouble" with many women.
"He was a very mysterious person," said the friend, who asked not to be identified.

# Moslem leader’s computer research led to controversy

By JOSEPH GARCIA
and CHARLOTTE LOWE
Citizen Staff Writers

Traffic slowed along North Euclid Avenue at the Islamic Mosque of Tucson yesterday as curious motorists tried to catch a glimpse past the yellow plastic tape used by police to cordon off the area.

The mosque, at 739 E. Sixth Ave., with its "Happiness Is Submission To God" message painted on the side of the building, was the site of a homicide.

Other than two dozen pairs of shoes left at the complex's doorstep, there was not much to see yesterday that was out of the ordinary.

While adults grieved inside, children played outside. Tears streamed from a youngster's eyes only after falling off the swing set. Two police officers standing nearby rushed to the child, handing him over to the parents, who then quickly went back inside, avoiding reporters.

Rashad Khalifa was dead.

Khalifa's followers, known as the "True Moslems," considered him the Messenger of God — the man who used a computer to decipher ancient codes of the Koran, the Moslem holy book.

The Arizona license plate of a car parked behind the mosque yesterday read, "ON IT 19," in reference to the numeral Khalifa said he used as the common denominator throughout his two decades of internationally known research.

The number 19 appeared too many times in the Koran to be a coincidence, he said. Through use of technology, he soon found patterns.

Khalifa, 54, said that without his Hewlett-Packard HP-1000. E-series computer, he — as countless others before him — would have lacked the essential tools in proving the Koran’s authenticity.

"The code shows that the Koran was written by God, not man," he said in a Tucson Citizen interview last year. "It is beyond human ability. It is his way of saying, 'I created this.' It is like a combination to a safe. It is a way to make contact with God."

He called himself "the Messenger of the Covenant," noting that his root name appears 19 times in the Koran and also is mentioned in Malachi, the last book of the Old Testament of the Bible.

The book Khalifa wrote, "The Computer Speaks: God's Message to the World," was printed by his own publishing company, Islamic Productions.

He also wrote "Quran: The Final Scripture (Authorized English Version)," said to be the first such translation of the Koran — or Quran, as he preferred — by a Muslim whose mother tongue is Arabic.

He said his 600-page English version is the only "authorized" one because it is "blessed" with his computer discoveries that broke the ancient code.

"This code," he said, "which remained a divinely guarded secret for 1,400 years, presents to the world the first physical, touchable and utterly indisputable proof that this Quran is a divine scripture."

The 1968 discovery earned him much notoriety in newspapers and magazines throughout the Middle East. His views also appeared in the September 1980 edition of the magazine Scientific American.

Khalifa's views were controversial. In 1978, when he was vice president of the Universty of Arizona's Islamic Center, he was physically removed from the congregation after voicing his opposition to stoning as punishment for

Throughout the years, Khalifa continued to espouse the idea that the majority of Moslems in the world were following customs and traditions -- not the actual teachings of the Koran.

**Muslim leader found slain in Tucson mosque**

**By Sam Negri**
*The Arizona Republic*

TUCSON — The leader of a Muslim splinter group was found slain Wednesday in a mosque near the University of Arizona, and police said he may have been killed because of his vehement criticism of traditional Muslims.

Rashad Khalifa, 54, was found by his secretary at 5:45 a.m. on the kitchen floor of the United Submitters International Mosque, according to Tucson police Detective Charles Armijo.

"He had several injuries," Armijo said, "and until an autopsy is performed, we aren't saying what the cause was. But we are considering this a homicide."

Armijo said police have not ruled out the possibility that Khalifa was the victim of warring factions within the Muslim community.

nity.

"An assistant of Khalifa, Edip Yuksel, reportedly said 'he was 90 percent sure' that Khalifa had been killed by assassins with religious ties."

Yuksel said Khalifa was prominent in Muslim circles and was not hard to find; coming to the mosque at 2 a.m. daily to study the Koran and to write, the *Tucson Citizen* reported Wednesday.

Yuksel told the newspaper, "He knew he would be killed" because of his research.

Police at first reported Khalifa had been shot.

A police spokesman later said "The type of trauma found on the body made it unclear what the cause of death was."

Investigators said there were no signs of forced entry and that the main door of the building was open when officers arrived.

Members of the mosque, who followed Khalifa's interpretation of the Koran, described themselves as "Submitters."

The extent of his following could not be determined immediately. Members of the congregation said they planned to issue a statement about their beliefs and the slaying.

The group's brochure states: "A Submitter is one who devotes his or her worship absolutely to God alone, no idols whatever. A Submitter may be Jewish, Christian, Muslim, Hindu, Buddhist, etc."

The brochure states most religions have strayed from their original teachings and that "today's Muslims have corrupted such teachings beyond recognition."

In an interview with the *Tucson Citizen* last year, Khalifa said he had proved through a 19-year computer study that all major components of the Koran, from verses to chapters, were composed in accordance with a mathematical code that had the common denominator of 19. He contended that the code proved that God, and not man wrote, the Koran, Islam's holy writings.

"The code proved that many Muslims were following satanic guidance. They were distorting the religion by following prophets that were men instead of God," Khalifa told the *Citizen*.

The newspaper reported that Khalifa, known as an imam, or prayer leader, was intent upon widely disseminating his discovery. He did so by transmitting his writings by facsimile machine throughout the Middle East.

Contributing to this report was *The Associated Press*.

NOT PREVIOUSLY IN RECEIPT OF RE PHOENIX TELS, THE FOLLOWING

CAPTIONED VICTIM WAS MURDERED ON JANUARY 31, 1990, SOMEWHERE
BETWEEN THE HOURS OF 2:00 AM, AND 5:00 PM, BY UNKNOWN ASSAILANTS
AT THE MASJID OR MOSQUE OF TUCSON, 729 EAST 6TH STREET, TUCSON,
ARIZONA. THE VICTIM WAS STABBED NUMEROUS TIMES IN BOTH THE CHEST
AND THE BACK AND HIS BODY WAS DRENCHED WITH A STRONG SOLVENT
CALLED "XYLOL". THE VICTIM WAS THE IMAN AND FOUNDER OF THE
MOSQUE OF TUCSON AND A MODERATE MOSLEM. HE WAS ALSO WELL KNOWN
AMONG MOSLEMS BECAUSE OF A COMPUTER ANALYSIS HE DID ON THE KORAN
THAT SHOWED A MATHEMATICAL FORMULA THAT HE BELIEVED PROVED THAT
THE KORAN IS THE DIVINE SCRIPTURE. THE VICTIM ALSO PUBLISHED A

**Subject:** Homicide investigation involving Tucson Police Department and militant Black Moslem sect Fugra

TRANSLATION OF THE KORAN IN WHICH HE OMITTED TWO VERSES HE

VICTIM WAS ALSO HATED BY MANY MAINSTREAM MOSLEMS FOR CLAIMING
THAT HE WAS THE MESSENGER OF GOD.

THE TUCSON POLICE DEPARTMENT (PD) REQUESTED THE FBI'S

BLACK MOSLEM SECT KNOWN AS FUGRA THAT MIGHT HAVE BEEN INVOLVED
WITH THE MURDER.  ADDITIONAL INFORMATION ON FUGRA CAN BE FOUND IN
RE DENVER TEL TO DIRECTOR AND ALL OFFICES, OCTOBER 30, 1989.

FUGRA THAT THEY HAVE RECEIVED FROM THE COLORADO SPRINGS PD.

ACCORDING TO THE TUCSON PD, ON SEPTEMBER 14, 1989, COLORADO
SPRINGS PD SEARCHED A STORAGE LOCKER BELONGING TO FUGRA.  INSIDE
THE STORAGE LOCKER, THE COLORADO SPRINGS PD DISCOVERED HANDGUNS
AND COMPONENTS TO MAKE EXPLOSIVES.  THEY ALSO FOUND MILITARY
TRAINING MANUALS, HANDWRITTEN NOTES ON BOMB CONSTRUCTION, AND A
SOMEWHAT DETAILED DESCRIPTION OF A BOMB ATTACK AGAINST A FACILITY

IN DENVER.  INSIDE THE STORAGE LOCKER WERE ALSO HANDWRITTEN

**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI Detroit | **Type:** Teletype
**Subject:** Fuqra Investigation, Detroit and Denver

PAGE TWO DE FBIDE 0016 UNCLAS E F T O

TWO DE [REDACTED: b3, b7E] UNCLAS E F T O

MUSLIM COMMUNITY HAVE NOT DEVELOPED ANY INFORMATION IN THIS REGARD. AS PREVIOUSLY REPORTED BY DETROIT, SUBSEQUENT TO THE HOMICIDE AND DOUBLE FIREBOMBING AT DETROIT, WHICH PRECIPITATED THE ORIGINAL INVESTIGATION OF THE FUQRA, OO: DETROIT, THE DETROIT FUQRA FACTION DISBANDED SHORTLY AFTER THE INCIDENT AND ARE BELIEVED TO HAVE RELOCATED OUTSIDE THE MICHIGAN AREA INASMUCH AS THE DETROIT FUQRA LEADER WAS LAST IDENTIFIED AS RESIDING IN KANSAS CITY. PRESENTLY, THE FUQRA ARE NOT KNOWN TO HAVE RE-ESTABLISHED THEMSELVES IN THE DETROIT METROPOLITAN AREA; HOWEVER, THIS DOES NOT PRECLUDE THE POSSIBLE EXISTENCE OF A COVERT FUQRA COMMUNITY WHICH HAS BEEN THEIR MODUS OPERANDI IN THE PAST.

DETROIT SOURCES HAVE BEEN ADVISED TO REMAIN ALERT AND PROMPTLY REPORT ANY INFORMATION THAT MAY BE OF ASSISTANCE TO CAPTIONED INVESTIGATION AND DENVER'S INVESTIGATION OF THE FUQRA.

**Subject:** Possible Terrorist Act; Rashad Khalifa Murder Investigation

ON JANUARY 13, 1990; POSSIBLE TERRORIST ACT; ACT-DT; 00:PX.

REFERENCE PHOENIX TELETYPES TO DIRECTOR, 2/2/90 AND 2/21/90.

REFERENCED PHOENIX TELETYPE ADVISED RECEIVING OFFICES THAT KHALIFA, THE IMAM AND THE FOUNDER OF THE MOSQUE IN TUCSON, ARIZONA, WAS WELL KNOWN AMONG MOSLEMS AND DEVELOPED A GREAT DEAL OF NOTORIETY BECAUSE OF HIS OUTSPOKEN THOUGHTS ON THE MOSLEM RELIGION AND HIS CLAIM THAT HE WAS THE MESSENGER OF GOD.

ON JANUARY 31, 1990 THE TUCSON POLICE DEPARTMENT REQUESTED THE FBI'S ASSISTANCE IN THIS KILLING BECAUSE IT BELIEVED THE HOMOCIDE COULD POSSIBLY HAVE BEEN COMMITTED FOR POLITICAL AND RELIGIOUS REASONS. OF OF THE GROUPS CONSIDERED SUSPECT IN THE INVESTIGATION WAS A BLACK MUSLIM SECT KNOWN AS FUQRA.

RECEIVING OFFICES WERE REQUESTED TO TASK LOGICAL ASSETS ON ANY INFORMATION RE FUQRA AND THE MURDER OF RASHAD KHALIFA.

FOR INFORMATION OF PHOENIX AND OTHER OFFICES, [REDACTED: b7D] AND [REDACTED] BOTH KNOWLEDGEABLE OF THE BLACK MUSLIM

## PAGE 103
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** Unknown | **Type:** Memo / Report
**Subject:** Investigation involving Fuqra and the Tucson Police Department

[REDACTED: b3, b7E]

Koran is a divine scripture. The victim also published a translation of the Koran in which he omitted two verses he believed were Satanic. Furthermore, the victim angered other Moslems by accusing them of following the Satanic Koran. The victim was also despised by many mainstream Moslems by claiming that he was a messenger of God.

The Tucson Police Department (TPD) requested the FBI's assistance in the homicide due to the possibility that the victim was slain for political or religious reasons. The TPD provided a packet of information dealing with a militant black Muslim sect known as Fuqra that may have been involved with the murder. Additional information concerning Fuqra can be found in referenced Denver teletype to Director and all offices dated 10/30/89. The TPD received the information on Fuqra from the Colorado Springs Police Department (CSPD) who searched a Fuqra storage locker on 9/14/89. Inside the storage locker, the CSPD discovered handguns and components to make explosives. They also found military training manuals, handwritten notes on bomb construction, and a somewhat detailed description of a bomb attack against a facility in Denver. Also inside the storage locker was handwritten instructional material dealing with various aspects of guerrilla warfare, terrorist bombings, sniping, target selection, and surveillance. A packet of target information was also found dealing with surveillances of potential targets in Denver, Colorado, Tucson, Arizona, and the Los Angeles area. Each of these packets contained detailed surveillance reports, diagrams, photographs, and identifying data on each target, as if it were being scouted for a terrorist attack.

A review of the Fuqra packet for Tucson revealed that there was a Mosque raid planned for the Tucson Mosque, supra. From a further review of this packet, it appears that KHALIFA was the probable target for this attack. This packet for Tucson was very detailed and included descriptions of the Mosque well as a stack of 35 millimeter color photographs of the Mosque from all angles, as well as interior shots. There were also details about the neighborhood, best methods to be used to kill the individual, and the best time to do it. One section of the package was also entitled Further Info for Final Setup. Under this information it says that a participant would go to the Mosque one week before the event, attend classes there, and determine the average crowd size as well as movements of the subject. Near the end of the plan is a section on "problems" and it states "He may not be

was drenched with a commercial solvent with trade name of Xylol

The owner of Apartment [REDACTED: b6, b7C] at [REDACTED: b6, b7C], Tucson,
Arizona, was contacted regarding [REDACTED: b6, b7C]. The owner advised
that [REDACTED: b6, b7C] rented the apartment on [REDACTED: b6, b7C] and paid cash. At
that time, [REDACTED: b6, b7C] asked the owner to drive him to the airport
to pick up his bags. [REDACTED: b6, b7C] had two duffel bags in a storage
locker at the airport. During their conversation, the owner said
that [REDACTED: b6, b7C] mentioned that he was from Oregon or Washington.

The next time the owner saw [REDACTED: b6, b7C] was on 1/16/90 when
he came to the owner's house to pay part of his rent. Again,
[REDACTED: b6, b7C] paid his rent in cash. On 2/6/90, the owner went to
[REDACTED: b6, b7C] apartment to collect rent due. From the looks of the
apartment, [REDACTED: b6, b7C] had moved out. It was the owner's opinion
that [REDACTED: b6, b7C] had never furnished the apartment. Interviews of
neighbors of [REDACTED: b6, b7C] could provide no additional information
about him.

Investigation by the New York office to date has
developed no information on [REDACTED: b6, b7C] or a [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

The following investigation was conducted by Special Agent (SA) [REDACTED] at San Bernardino, California: [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

On December 13, 1989, Senior Deputy [REDACTED] Intelligence Analysis/Technical Services, SAN BERNARDINO COUNTY SHERIFF'S DEPARTMENT (SBCSD), 655 East Third Street, San Bernardino, California, 92415, telephone [REDACTED] advised SA [REDACTED] that a review of their case number [REDACTED] indicated an individual named [REDACTED] also known as [REDACTED] male, date of birth [REDACTED] pounds, [REDACTED] hair, [REDACTED] eyes, address [REDACTED] California, [REDACTED] was charged with [REDACTED] (California Penal Code [REDACTED]), following an incident which took place on [REDACTED]. The victim in that matter, [REDACTED] California, telephone [REDACTED] was dirt bike riding with [REDACTED] on property that [REDACTED] owned. [REDACTED] stated that while approaching an abandoned wooden shack on his property, approximately three or four shots were fired at him, apparently from the shack. [REDACTED] confronted six [REDACTED] individuals at that location, some of whom were wearing clothing similar to that worn in Far Eastern countries. An individual later identified as [REDACTED] claimed that he was firing warning shots at them to keep them off the property, as the property was owned by a perosn known to [REDACTED] tried to explain, to no avail, that in fact the property was owned by [REDACTED].

On November 13, 1988, [REDACTED] was confronted by an SBCSD deputy regarding this matter. [REDACTED] was extremely beligerent and challenged the deputy's authority. [REDACTED] stated that if the deputy wanted to talk to him, the deputy should arrest him. [REDACTED] stated that the deputy was on Moslem land, and that he did not have to abide by "white supremacist pig laws". The following investigation was conducted by [REDACTED: SA] Los Angeles Division:

On 3/20/90, physical observation of 1274 S. Waterman Avenue, San Bernardino, California, disclosed that this is a single story building containing possibly ten to fifteen small, one room suites.  A sign in the front yard indicates that "private offices" were available there from $129 a month, through telephone 714/884-7211.

The readerboard in the hallway of this building indicated that this is the VALLEY BUSINESS PARK, and suite 118 is occupied by SOUTHERN CALIFORNIA LANDSCAPING.

The readerboard had no listing for [REDACTED] or the IBN BATTUTA FOUNDATION.

The door bearing 118 was closed and had no other identifiers on it.

On March 23, 1990, Investigative Assistant [REDACTED] obtained four copies of a photograph of [REDACTED] taken from the Riverside County Sheriff's Department, Riverside, California.

On March 23, 1990, [REDACTED] (protect identity) California, telephone [REDACTED] advised Special Agent (SA) [REDACTED: b6, b7C, b7D]

A check with the Social Security office in Tucson, Arizona disclosed that 3808 West Clarendon is the Social Security office located in West Oak Cliff, Texas, which is a suburb of Dallas. It was also disclosed that this type of receipt is not usually kept on the counter, but would be monitored by someone on duty.

## PAGE 200
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI Detroit | **Type:** Teletype
**Subject:** Fuqra Activity in Detroit Area / Investigation

PAGE TWO DE FBIDE 0005 UNCLAS E F T O

[REDACTED] AT [REDACTED] A REVIEW OF THE RECORDS AT THE MICHIGAN BUREAU OF VITAL STATISTICS, LANSING, MICHIGAN, WAS NEGATIVE. APPARENTLY THE CERTIFICATE WAS A FORGERY INASMUCH AS DALLAS TELETYPE, JUNE 23, 1990, NOTED THAT IN NOVEMBER, 1989, AT DALLAS, TEXAS, [REDACTED] APPLIED FOR A SOCIAL SECURITY NUMBER USING A [REDACTED] BIRTH CERTIFICATE THAT WAS LATER DETERMINED TO BE FRAUDULENT.

THE COLOR PHOTOGRAPH OF [REDACTED] PROVIDED IN REFERENCED AIRTEL, HAS PERIODICALLY BEEN DISPLAYED TO [REDACTED] A RE-ACTIVATED BLACK MUSLIM SOURCE FAMILIAR WITH THE FUQRA COMMUNITY; HOWEVER, BOTH THE INDIVIDUAL DEPICTED IN THE PHOTOGRAPH AND THE NAME [REDACTED] ARE UNKNOWN TO THE SOURCE.

FOR THE INFORMATION OF PHOENIX, IN MARCH, 1990, DETROIT RE-INSTITUTED ITS INVESTIGATION OF THE FUQRA (FULL DS/T INVESTIGATION AUTHORIZED, MARCH 12, 1990, OO: DENVER), IN ORDER TO DETERMINE THE SCOPE AND NATURE OF WHAT APPEARS TO BE A RESURGENCE OF FUQRA ACTIVITY IN THE DETROIT AREA. IN THIS REGARD, DETROIT WILL KEEP PHOENIX APPRISED OF ANY DEVELOPMENTS THAT MAY IMPACT ON CAPTIONED INVESTIGATION.

The following investigation was conducted by Special Agent (SA) [REDACTED] at Cincinnati, Ohio:

On May 16, 1990, computer checks were conducted at the Cincinnati Office of the [REDACTED] having date of birth [REDACTED] and utilizing Social Security Account Numbers (SSANS) [REDACTED] and [REDACTED] Checks were conducted of Triple I (Interstate Identification Index), the Ohio State Department of Motor Vehicles (Columbus, Ohio), and the Regional Crime Information Center (RCIC) for Hamilton County (Cincinnati). All such checks met with negative results. [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

On May 21, 1990, information concerning the murder of RASHAD ABDEL KHALIFA at Tucson, Arizona, on January 31, 1990, was provided to Specialist [REDACTED] Investigator, Cincinnati Police Division, Intelligence Section, Cincinnati, Ohio, telephone number [REDACTED]. Subsequent record check with the Cincinnati Police Department met with negative results. [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

On June 13, 1990, a Letterhead Memorandum (LHM) was prepared which summarized, in very general detail, information concerning the murder of RASHAD ABDEL KHALIFA. These general details included the date of the murder and location, the method utilized [REDACTED] and the possible motive. The suspect, [REDACTED] was described. Copies of this LHM (suitable for dissemination) was provided to all Agents within the Cincinnati Division to ensure informant coverage and was also provided to the police departments of the metropolitan areas of Dayton and Columbus, Ohio. [REDACTED: b6, b7C]

[REDACTED: b6, b7C] indicated he would immediately notify the FBI if he became aware of any threats against the Ahmadiyya Movement by the FUQRA group. [REDACTED: b3, b6, b7C, b7E]

**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** Information Management Division | **Type:** Letter

For questions regarding our determinations, visit the www.fbi.gov/foia website under “Contact Us.”

If you are not satisfied with the Federal Bureau of Investigation’s determination in response to this request,
you may administratively appeal by writing to the Director, Office of Information Policy (OIP), United States
Department of Justice, 441 G Street, NW, 6th Floor, Washington, D.C. 20530, or you may submit an appeal through
OIP's FOIPA STAR portal by creating an account following the instructions on OIP's website:
https://www.justice.gov/oip/submit-and-track-request-or-appeal. Your appeal must be postmarked or electronically
transmitted within ninety (90) days of the date of my response to your request. If you submit your appeal by mail,
both the letter and the envelope should be clearly marked "Freedom of Information Act Appeal." Please cite the
FOIPA Request Number assigned to your request so it may be easily identified.

You may seek dispute resolution services by contacting the Office of Government Information Services
(OGIS). The contact information for OGIS is as follows: Office of Government Information Services, National
Archives and Records Administration, 8601 Adelphi Road-OGIS, College Park, Maryland 20740-6001, e-mail at
ogis@nara.gov; telephone at 202-741-5770; toll free at 1-877-684-6448; or facsimile at 202-741-5769. Alternatively,

resolution correspondence by email, the subject heading should clearly state “Dispute Resolution Services.” Please
also cite the FOIPA Request Number assigned to your request so it may be easily identified.

Enclosure(s)

In response to your negotiated FOIA request, enclosed are the processed documents. This is the **second
interim release** of information responsive to your FOIA request. Duplicate copies of the same document were not
processed. This material is being provided to you at no charge.

Based on the information you provided, we conducted a main and reference entity record search of the
Central Records System (CRS) per our standard search policy. For more information about records searches and
the standard search policy, see the enclosed FBI FOIPA Addendum General Information Section.

As previously indicated, documents were located which originated with, or contained information concerning,
other agencies. We are consulting with the other agencies and are awaiting their response. Our office has
processed all other information currently in our possession. The FBI will correspond with you regarding those
documents when the consultation is completed.

## PAGE 3
**Date:** Unknown | **Origin:** FBI | **Type:** Addendum
**Subject:** FBI FOIA Addendum

FBI FOIA Addendum

As referenced in our letter responding to your Freedom of Information/Privacy Acts (FOIPA) request, the FBI FOIPA Addendum provides information applicable to your request. Part 1 of the Addendum includes standard responses that apply to all requests. Part 2 includes standard responses that apply to requests for records about individuals to the extent your request seeks the listed information. Part 3 includes general information about FBI records, searches, and programs.

**Part 1: The standard responses below apply to all requests:**

(i) **5 U.S.C. § 552(c).** Congress excluded three categories of law enforcement and national security records from the requirements of the FOIPA [5 U.S.C. § 552(c)]. FBI responses are limited to those records subject to the requirements of the FOIPA. Additional information about the FBI and the FOIPA can be found on the www.fbi.gov/foia website.

(ii) **Intelligence Records.** To the extent your request seeks records of intelligence sources, methods, or activities, the FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of records pursuant to FOIA exemptions (b)(1), (b)(3), and as applicable to requests for records about individuals, PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(1), (b)(3), and (j)(2)]. The mere acknowledgment of the existence or nonexistence of such records is itself a classified fact protected by FOIA exemption (b)(1) and/or would reveal intelligence sources, methods, or activities protected by exemption (b)(3) [50 USC § 3024(i)(1)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that any such records do or do not exist.

**Part 2: The standard responses below apply to all requests for records on individuals:**

(i) **Requests for Records about any Individual—Watch Lists.** The FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of any individual's name on a watch list pursuant to FOIA exemption (b)(7)(E) and PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(7)(E), (j)(2)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that watch list records do or do not exist.

(ii) **Requests for Records about any Individual—Witness Security Program Records.** The FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of records which could identify any participant in the Witness Security Program pursuant to FOIA exemption (b)(3) and PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(3), 18 U.S.C. 3521, and (j)(2)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that such records do or do not exist.

(iii) **Requests for Records for Incarcerated Individuals.** The FBI can neither confirm nor deny the existence of records which could reasonably be expected to endanger the life or physical safety of any incarcerated individual pursuant to FOIA exemptions (b)(7)(E), (b)(7)(F), and PA exemption (j)(2) [5 U.S.C. §§ 552/552a (b)(7)(E), (b)(7)(F), and (j)(2)]. This is a standard response and should not be read to indicate that such records do or do not exist.

**Part 3: General Information:**

(i) **Record Searches.** The Record/Information Dissemination Section (RIDS) searches for reasonably described records by searching systems or locations where responsive records would reasonably be found. A standard search normally consists of a search for main files in the Central Records System (CRS), an extensive system of records consisting of applicant, investigative, intelligence, personnel, administrative, and general files compiled by the FBI per its law enforcement, intelligence, and administrative functions. The CRS spans the entire FBI organization, comprising records of FBI Headquarters, FBI Field Offices, and FBI Legal Attaché Offices (Legats) worldwide; Electronic Surveillance (ELSUR) records are included in the CRS. Unless specifically requested, a standard search does not include references, administrative records of previous FOIPA requests, or civil litigation files. For additional information about our record searches, visit www.fbi.gov/services/information-management/foipa/requesting-fbi-records.

(ii) **FBI Records.** Founded in 1908, the FBI carries out a dual law enforcement and national security mission. As part of this dual mission, the FBI creates and maintains records on various subjects; however, the FBI does not maintain records on every person, subject, or entity.

(iii) **Requests for Criminal History Records or Rap Sheets.** The Criminal Justice Information Services (CJIS) Division provides Identity History Summary Checks – often referred to as a criminal history record or rap sheet. These criminal history records are not the same as material in an investigative “FBI file.” An Identity History Summary Check is a listing of information taken from fingerprint cards and documents submitted to the FBI in connection with arrests, federal employment, naturalization, or military service. For a fee, individuals can request a copy of their Identity History Summary Check. Forms and directions can be accessed at www.fbi.gov/about-us/cjis/identity-history-summary-checks. Additionally, requests can be submitted electronically at www.edo.cjis.gov. For additional information, please contact CJIS directly at (304) 625-5500.

(iv) **National Name Check Program (NNCP).** The mission of NNCP is to analyze and report information in response to name check requests received from federal agencies, for the purpose of protecting the United States from foreign and domestic threats to national security. Please be advised that this is a service provided to other federal agencies. Private Citizens cannot request a name check.
